Though that would be a shift in how the tiers are supposed to work, so I need to think through whether that's the right call.\n\nMichael's asking me to dig deeper into what's actually broken with Kilo T1. He knows Dialagram is down, but he's confused about why Kilo T1 isn't working when used inline. Looking at what he said — \"I am currently using Kilo Free Auto under their paid subscription, I am currently using it inline with Kilo, this is the problem\" — I think the real issue is that OB-1 should be dispatching directly to Kilo through the CLI (PCClaw), not making an API call through PA that times out. The dispatch chain is going PA → API call → timeout when it should be going straight through PCClaw to Kilo's CLI for a fast inline response.\n\nNow I'm reconsidering what PCClaw actually is. The description says \"via PA T1\" which sounds like it would have the same timeout problem, but maybe PCClaw is a Kilo skill that runs locally on Michael's machine. If that's the case, then when OB-1 invokes PCClaw, it fires as a local Kilo agent task — meaning Kilo handles the generation locally without hitting the CF 30-second wall. The \"via PA T1\" part might just be for logging and status tracking, not the actual generation path itself.\n\nSo the real distinction is: PCClaw represents the local T1 path using Kilo directly, while PA's `/api/code` endpoint is the remote T1 path that goes through the CloudFlare worker and times out. The fix Michael's suggesting is to use PCClaw for T1 dispatches instead of the remote PA API call, though CLAUDE.md currently forces all T1 work through PA. The better architecture would route simple tasks through PCClaw locally and reserve PA's remote API for complex multi-file work once the backend is actually working.\n\nLooking at the actual dispatch chain that's functioning now, T1 always times out because Kilo's free tier is too slow, so it falls back to T10 which works but only when given exact old/new strings—it can't discover changes on its own since those models can't access the filesystem. The real bottleneck isn't the dispatch logic itself, it's that T10 needs explicit diffs to function properly.\n\nThe fix needs to happen at the PA level upstream.
